   656	def _resolve_branch(root: Path, branch: str) -> str | None:
   657	    """D8.4: the oracle branch as a REF — the local head first, else its
   658	    origin/ tracking form (a fresh clone that fetched the preserved line
   659	    must lint identically). Deliberately not a bare rev-parse: that
   660	    would accept a tag or a raw sha as a 'branch'."""
   661	    for ref in (f"refs/heads/{branch}", f"refs/remotes/origin/{branch}"):
   662	        out = subprocess.run(
   663	            ["git", "-C", str(root), "rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", ref],
   664	            capture_output=True,
   665	            text=True,
   666	        )
   667	        if out.returncode == 0 and out.stdout.strip():
   668	            return out.stdout.strip()
   669	    return None
   670	
   671	
   672	def check_superseded_record(path: Path, block: object, checker: Checker) -> None:
   673	    """D8.3–D8.5: the archival record's shape AND its pointers. Every
   674	    field is a checkable claim about the world, checked here — the
   675	    record is the machine lock, and a lock whose pointers do not
   676	    resolve is broken, LOUD, never a skip."""
   677	    name = path.name
   678	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or set(block.keys()) != SUPERSEDED_KEYS:
   679	        checker.error(
   680	            f"{name}: superseded record must be an object with exactly keys "
   681	            f"{sorted(SUPERSEDED_KEYS)} — no successor field: the successor "
   682	            f"does not exist at flip time and an unverifiable reference is "
   683	            f"the defect family this record exists to avoid"
   684	        )
   685	        return
   686	    if not isinstance(block["date"], str) or not DATE_RE.match(block["date"]):
   687	        checker.error(f"{name}: superseded.date must be a YYYY-MM-DD string")
   688	
   689	    located = git_root_and_rel(path)
   690	    if located is None:
   691	        checker.error(
   692	            f"{name}: not inside a git repository — the superseded record's "
   693	            f"oracle pointers cannot be verified"
   694	        )
   695	        return
   696	    root, _rel = located
   697	
   698	    # D8.5: the forward thread must resolve where it is written — the
   699	    # citation rule (an unverified citation is the citation-shaped form
   700	    # of an unrun "measured") applied to the record's own pointer.
   701	    plan = block["plan"]
   702	    if not isinstance(plan, str) or not plan.strip() or plan.startswith("/") or ".." in plan:
   703	        checker.error(
   704	            f"{name}: superseded.plan must be a nonempty repo-relative path "
   705	            f"(no absolute paths, no '..')"
   706	        )
   707	    elif not (root / plan).is_file():
   708	        checker.error(
   709	            f"{name}: superseded.plan path '{plan}' does not exist — the "
   710	            f"record's forward thread must resolve at the point of writing"
   711	        )
   712	
   713	    branch = block["oracle_branch"]
   714	    branch_sha = None
   715	    if not isinstance(branch, str) or not branch.strip():
   716	        checker.error(f"{name}: superseded.oracle_branch must be a nonempty string")
   717	    else:
   718	        branch_sha = _resolve_branch(root, branch)
   719	        if branch_sha is None:
   720	            checker.error(
   721	                f"{name}: superseded.oracle_branch '{branch}' does not resolve to "
   722	                f"a branch (neither refs/heads nor refs/remotes/origin) — the "
   723	                f"preserved line is unreachable and the archival record is broken"
   724	            )
   725	
   726	    tip = block["oracle_tip"]
   727	    if not isinstance(tip, str) or not FULL_SHA_RE.match(tip):
   728	        checker.error(
   729	            f"{name}: superseded.oracle_tip must be a 40-hex lowercase commit sha "
   730	            f"(pinned FULL — an abbreviation is a prefix claim)"
   731	        )
   732	        return
   733	    typ = subprocess.run(
   734	        ["git", "-C", str(root), "cat-file", "-t", tip],
   735	        capture_output=True,
   736	        text=True,
   737	    )
   738	    obj_type = typ.stdout.strip() if typ.returncode == 0 else "unresolvable"
   739	    if obj_type != "commit":
   740	        checker.error(
   741	            f"{name}: superseded.oracle_tip '{tip[:12]}' does not resolve to a "
   742	            f"COMMIT (got '{obj_type}') — the archival record is broken"
   743	        )
   744	    elif branch_sha is not None:
   745	        contained = subprocess.run(
   746	            ["git", "-C", str(root), "merge-base", "--is-ancestor", tip, branch_sha],
   747	            capture_output=True,
   748	        )
   749	        if contained.returncode != 0:
   750	            checker.error(
   751	                f"{name}: superseded.oracle_tip '{tip[:12]}' is NOT contained in "
   752	                f"branch '{branch}' — the record names a tip the preserved line "
   753	                f"does not carry"
   754	            )
   755	
   756	
   757	def check_draft(path: Path, checker: Checker) -> dict | None:
   758	    """Returns {"status": str, "rows": set[str]} for cross-ref use, or None."""
   759	    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   760	    blocks = json_blocks(text, path.name, checker)
   761	
   762	    metas = block_by_key(blocks, "contract_draft")
   763	    if len(metas) != 1:
   764	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: expected exactly one contract_draft block, found {len(metas)}")
   765	        return None
   766	    meta = metas[0]
   767	    if not isinstance(meta, dict):
   768	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: contract_draft is not an object")
   769	        return None
   770	    if set(meta.keys()) != CONTRACT_DRAFT_KEYS:
   771	        checker.error(
   772	            f"{path.name}: contract_draft must be an object with exactly keys "
   773	            f"{sorted(CONTRACT_DRAFT_KEYS)}"
   774	        )
   775	    status = meta.get("status")
   776	    chapter = meta.get("chapter")
   777	    surface = meta.get("surface")
   778	    if status not in DRAFT_STATUSES:
   779	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: contract_draft.status '{status}' not in {DRAFT_STATUSES}")
   780	        return None
   781	
   782	    name_match = DRAFT_NAME_RE.match(path.name)
   783	    if not name_match:
   784	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: filename must be ch<N>-<surface>-contract.md")
   785	    elif chapter != name_match.group(1) or surface != name_match.group(2):
   786	        checker.error(
   787	            f"{path.name}: contract_draft chapter/surface ({chapter}/{surface}) "
   788	            f"does not match filename"
   789	        )
   790	
   791	    rows_list = [C_ROW_RE.match(line).group(1) for line in c_row_lines(text)]  # type: ignore[union-attr]
   792	    dupes = {r for r in rows_list if rows_list.count(r) > 1}
   793	    if dupes:
   794	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: duplicate C-row ids {sorted(dupes)}")
   795	    # The lane-id no-leading-zeros rule's sibling surface (the round-5
   796	    # family, swept): detection stays broad (a demoted C01 row would
   797	    # silently escape the equality guard), validation is explicit.
   798	    zero_ids = sorted({r for r in rows_list if r.startswith("C0")})
   799	    if zero_ids:
   800	        checker.error(
   801	            f"{path.name}: leading-zero C-row ids {zero_ids} — ids are exact "
   802	            f"strings (C01 is never C1), so leading-zero forms are forbidden"
   803	        )
   804	    rows = set(rows_list)
   805	
   806	    ratifications = block_by_key(blocks, "ratification")
   807	    for index, block in enumerate(ratifications):
   808	        check_ratification_shape(path.name, index, block, checker)
   809	    dates = [b["date"] for b in ratifications if isinstance(b, dict) and isinstance(b.get("date"), str)]
   810	    for prev, cur in zip(dates, dates[1:]):
   811	        if DATE_RE.match(prev) and DATE_RE.match(cur) and cur < prev:
   812	            checker.error(
   813	                f"{path.name}: ratification dates decrease in document order "
   814	                f"('{prev}' then '{cur}') — the latest block is the LAST block, "
   815	                f"and dates must be non-decreasing"
   816	            )
   817	            break
   818	
   819	    # D4 state consistency — decidable from the current bytes alone.
   820	    if status == "draft" and ratifications:
   821	        checker.error(
   822	            f"{path.name}: status draft with {len(ratifications)} ratification "
   823	            f"block(s) — draft implies no blocks (blocks <=> ratified-or-later "
   824	            f"incl. reopened)"
   825	        )
   826	    if status != "draft" and not ratifications:
   827	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: status {status} requires a ratification block")
   828	    if status in EQUALITY_BOUND and not rows:
   829	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: status {status} but no C-rows")
   830	
   831	    # D8 the superseded record — ANY record present <=> status
   832	    # superseded, both directions; the pointers verified where written.
   833	    supersedes = block_by_key(blocks, "superseded")
   834	    if supersedes and status != "superseded":
   835	        checker.error(
   836	            f"{path.name}: superseded record present on status '{status}' — the "
   837	            f"record and the status flip land in ONE commit"
   838	        )
   839	    if status == "superseded":
   840	        if len(supersedes) != 1:
   950	        elif row not in draft["rows"]:
   951	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — row {row} not in draft")
   952	        return
   953	    m = ADR_STRICT_RE.match(ref)
   954	    if m:
   955	        if not list(adr_dir.glob(f"ADR-{m.group(1)}-*.md")):
   956	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — no such file in {adr_dir.name}/")
   957	        return
   958	    checker.error(
   959	        f"{label} '{ref}' is neither a strict form "
   960	        f"(contract:ch<N>-<surface>#C<n>, ADR-NNN) nor prose:-prefixed — "
   961	        f"in declared machine data a near-miss is loud, never reinterpreted"
   962	    )
   963	
   964	
   965	def check_packet_rows(
   966	    name: str,
   967	    blocks: list[dict],
   968	    prose: str,
   969	    drafts: dict[str, dict],
   970	    adr_dir: Path,
   971	    checker: Checker,
   972	) -> dict[str, int] | None:
   973	    """P3-P5. Returns the provenance tally (underscore keys) or None."""
   974	    rows_blocks = block_by_key(blocks, "packet_rows")
   975	    if len(rows_blocks) != 1:
   976	        checker.error(f"{name}: v2 packet requires exactly one packet_rows block, found {len(rows_blocks)}")
   977	        return None
   978	    manifest = rows_blocks[0]
   979	    if not isinstance(manifest, dict) or set(manifest.keys()) != PACKET_ROWS_KEYS:
   980	        checker.error(f"{name}: packet_rows must be an object with exactly keys {sorted(PACKET_ROWS_KEYS)}")
   981	        return None
   982	    rows = manifest["rows"]
   983	    if not isinstance(rows, list):
   984	        checker.error(f"{name}: packet_rows.rows must be a list")
   985	        return None
   986	
   987	    tally = {"anchored": 0, "derived": 0, "new_decision": 0}
   988	    manifest_ids: list[str] = []
   989	    for row in rows:
   990	        if not isinstance(row, dict) or set(row.keys()) != ROW_KEYS:
   991	            checker.error(f"{name}: every packet_rows row must be an object with exactly keys {sorted(ROW_KEYS)}")
   992	            continue
   993	        row_id = row["id"]
   994	        m = MANIFEST_ID_RE.match(row_id) if isinstance(row_id, str) else None
   995	        if m is None:
   996	            checker.error(
   997	                f"{name}: row id '{row_id}' is not lane-grammar shaped "
   998	                f"([A-Z]{{1,2}} + integer, no leading zeros)"
   999	            )
  1000	            continue
  1001	        if m.group(1) in EXCLUDED_LANE_FAMILIES:
  1002	            checker.error(
  1003	                f"{name}: row id '{row_id}' uses reserved lane family "
  1004	                f"'{m.group(1)}' (mirrored from task-packet-template.md §1)"
  1005	            )
  1006	            continue
  1007	        normalized = row_id  # exact string — no numeric normalization
  1008	        manifest_ids.append(normalized)
  1009	        cls = row["class"]
  1010	        refs = row["refs"]
  1011	        if cls not in ROW_CLASSES:
  1012	            checker.error(f"{name}: row {normalized} class '{cls}' not in {ROW_CLASSES}")
  1013	            continue
  1014	        if not isinstance(refs, list):
  1015	            checker.error(f"{name}: row {normalized} refs must be a list")
  1016	            continue
  1017	        if cls == "new-decision" and refs:
  1018	            checker.error(
  1019	                f"{name}: row {normalized} is new-decision but carries refs — "
  1020	                f"the class is ref-less by definition (nothing prior determines it)"
  1021	            )
  1022	        if cls in ("anchored", "derived") and not refs:
  1023	            checker.error(f"{name}: row {normalized} is {cls} but carries no ref")
  1024	        for ref in refs:
  1025	            check_ref(name, normalized, ref, drafts, adr_dir, checker)
  1026	        tally[cls.replace("-", "_")] += 1
  1027	
  1028	    dupes = {i for i in manifest_ids if manifest_ids.count(i) > 1}
  1029	    if dupes:
  1030	        checker.error(f"{name}: duplicate packet_rows ids {sorted(dupes)}")
  1031	
  1032	    # P5 bidirectional completeness — the ONLY document scan, and it
  1033	    # reads nothing but first-cell lane ids outside fences.
  1034	    doc_id_list: list[str] = []
  1035	    for line in prose.splitlines():
  1036	        m = LANE_DEF_RE.match(line)
  1037	        if m and m.group(1) not in EXCLUDED_LANE_FAMILIES:
  1038	            doc_id_list.append(f"{m.group(1)}{m.group(2)}")  # exact string — O01 is never O1
  1039	    doc_dupes = sorted({i for i in doc_id_list if doc_id_list.count(i) > 1})
  1040	    if doc_dupes:
  1041	        checker.error(
  1042	            f"{name}: table-defined lane ids {doc_dupes} appear more than once — "
  1043	            f"canonical row ids are unique, and a duplicate makes every ref to "
  1044	            f"them ambiguous"
  1045	        )
  1046	    doc_ids = set(doc_id_list)
  1047	    manifest_set = set(manifest_ids)
  1048	    for missing in sorted(manifest_set - doc_ids):
  1049	        checker.error(
  1050	            f"{name}: manifest row {missing} is not defined as a table row in the "
  1051	            f"document (fenced code does not count)"
  1052	        )
  1053	    for missing in sorted(doc_ids - manifest_set):
  1054	        checker.error(
  1055	            f"{name}: table-defined lane id {missing} is missing from the "
  1056	            f"packet_rows manifest — the manifest is the authoritative registry"
  1057	        )
  1058	    return tally
  1059	
  1060	
  1061	def check_ref_closure(name: str, blocks: list[dict], prose: str, checker: Checker) -> None:
  1062	    """P10 (ch12 boundary — the P1b ref-drift class): prose closures vs
  1063	    the machine face. Skips silently when the manifest is malformed —
  1064	    P3's errors already own that."""
  1065	    rows_blocks = block_by_key(blocks, "packet_rows")
  1066	    if len(rows_blocks) != 1 or not isinstance(rows_blocks[0], dict):
  1067	        return
  1068	    rows = rows_blocks[0].get("rows")
  1069	    if not isinstance(rows, list):
  1070	        return
  1071	    manifest_refs: set[str] = set()
  1072	    for row in rows:
  1073	        if isinstance(row, dict) and isinstance(row.get("refs"), list):
  1074	            manifest_refs.update(r for r in row["refs"] if isinstance(r, str))
  1075	    header_union: dict[str, set[str]] = {}
  1076	    for m in HEADER_UNION_RE.finditer(prose):
  1077	        header_union.setdefault(m.group(1), set()).update(
  1078	            HEADER_UNION_ROW_RE.findall(m.group(2))
  1079	        )
  1080	    for closure in CLOSURE_RE.finditer(prose):
  1081	        kind = closure.group(1)
  1082	        for tok in CONTRACT_TOKEN_RE.finditer(closure.group(2)):
  1083	            full = f"contract:{tok.group(1)}#{tok.group(2)}"
  1084	            if full not in manifest_refs:
  1085	                checker.error(
  1086	                    f"{name}: '{full}' cited in a ({kind}: …) closure but missing "
  1087	                    f"from the packet_rows manifest refs — the prose↔manifest "
  1088	                    f"ref-drift class (ch12 boundary)"
  1089	                )
  1090	            if kind == "anchored":
  1091	                surface, row_c = tok.group(1), tok.group(2)
  1092	                if surface in header_union and row_c not in header_union[surface]:
  1093	                    checker.error(
  1094	                        f"{name}: anchored ref '{full}' missing from the header "
  1095	                        f"`contract:{surface}` rows union — the header-union "
  1096	                        f"drift class (ch12 boundary)"
  1097	                    )
  1098	
  1099	
  1100	def check_mirror_map(
  1101	    name: str,
  1102	    text: str,
  1103	    blocks: list[dict],
  1104	    prose: str,
  1105	    checker: Checker,
  1106	) -> None:
  1107	    """P11: the pointer-only register. Gates ONLY declaring packets —
  1108	    a packet without the mirror_map block is untouched (declaration
  1109	    grandfathering); the silent-demotion hole is closed textually like
  1110	    P1 (naming the block with a malformed fence is red, not a skip).
  1111	    Every check compares declared literals against bytes: register
  1112	    shape, canonical existence, pointer resolution, and signature
  1113	    confinement (a signature string may occur only on its canonical
  1114	    line, an anchor-bearing line, or an allow-listed line — pointer-
  1115	    bearing lines are deliberately NOT exempt: a restatement beside
  1116	    its own pointer is the loophole that exemption would open)."""
  1117	    maps = block_by_key(blocks, "mirror_map")
  1118	    if not maps:
  1119	        # The anti-demotion guard reads MACHINE-BLOCK raws only (arm
  1120	        # finding 10): a prose mention or a markdown-quoted example of
  1121	        # the identifier must not opt a packet in, while a malformed
  1122	        # declaration fence still must not silently un-gate.
  1123	        if any('"mirror_map"' in raw for raw in _scan_fences(text)[1]):
  1124	            checker.error(
  1125	                f"{name}: names mirror_map but no parseable mirror_map "
  1126	                f"block found — a malformed register must not silently "
  1127	                f"un-gate the pointer dimension"
  1128	            )
  1129	        return
  1130	    if len(maps) > 1:
  1131	        checker.error(f"{name}: {len(maps)} mirror_map blocks; exactly one allowed")
  1132	    mirror = maps[0]
  1133	    if not isinstance(mirror, dict) or set(mirror.keys()) != MIRROR_MAP_KEYS:
  1134	        checker.error(
  1135	            f"{name}: mirror_map must be an object with exactly keys "
  1136	            f"{sorted(MIRROR_MAP_KEYS)}"
  1137	        )
  1138	        return
  1139	    if mirror["form"] != "pointer-only":
  1140	        checker.error(
  1141	            f"{name}: mirror_map.form '{mirror['form']}' — the one "
  1142	            f"declared form is 'pointer-only' (future forms arrive by "
  1143	            f"extending this check, never by silent acceptance)"
  1144	        )
  1145	        return
  1146	    rules = mirror["rules"]
  1147	    if not isinstance(rules, list) or not rules:
  1148	        checker.error(f"{name}: mirror_map.rules must be a nonempty list")
  1149	        return
  1150	
  1151	    # The P5 scan's table-defined lane ids — canonical targets.
  1152	    # Reserved lane families are excluded exactly as P5 excludes them
  1153	    # (arm finding 4): a packet-title-style P-row is never a canonical.
  1154	    doc_ids: set[str] = set()
  1155	    for line in prose.splitlines():
  1156	        m = LANE_DEF_RE.match(line)
  1157	        if m and m.group(1) not in EXCLUDED_LANE_FAMILIES:
  1158	            doc_ids.add(f"{m.group(1)}{m.group(2)}")
  1159	
  1160	    prose_lines = prose.splitlines()
  1161	
  1162	    # Headings + their section ranges, for anchor-slug validation
  1163	    # (arm finding 2): a slug must name a real heading whose section
  1164	    # holds the token, or the canonical points readers nowhere. A
  1165	    # section runs to the next heading of the SAME OR HIGHER level —
  1166	    # child subsections belong to their parent (arm re-run finding 3).
  1167	    headings: list[tuple[int, int, str]] = []
  1168	    for i, ln in enumerate(prose_lines):
  1169	        hm = MIRROR_HEADING_RE.match(ln)
  1170	        if hm:
  1171	            headings.append((i, len(hm.group(1)), _mirror_kebab(hm.group(2))))
  1172	
  1173	    def slug_sections(slug: str) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
  1174	        spans: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
  1175	        for idx, (start, level, kebab) in enumerate(headings):
  1176	            if kebab == slug or kebab.startswith(slug + "-"):
  1177	                end = len(prose_lines)
  1178	                for nstart, nlevel, _nk in headings[idx + 1 :]:
  1179	                    if nlevel <= level:
  1180	                        end = nstart
  1181	                        break
  1182	                spans.append((start, end))
  1183	        # A matched CHILD heading nested inside a matched parent's span
  1184	        # is the same home, not an ambiguity — keep outermost spans
  1185	        # only; the ambiguity error is for DISJOINT matches.
  1186	        return [
  1187	            s
  1188	            for s in spans
  1189	            if not any(o != s and o[0] <= s[0] and s[1] <= o[1] for o in spans)
  1190	        ]
  1191	    rule_ids: list[str] = []
  1192	    checked_rules: list[dict] = []
  1193	    for rule in rules:
  1194	        if not isinstance(rule, dict) or set(rule.keys()) != MIRROR_RULE_KEYS:
  1195	            checker.error(
  1196	                f"{name}: every mirror_map rule must be an object with "
  1197	                f"exactly keys {sorted(MIRROR_RULE_KEYS)}"
  1198	            )
  1199	            continue
  1200	        rid = rule["id"]
  1201	        if not isinstance(rid, str) or not MIRROR_RULE_ID_RE.match(rid):
  1202	            checker.error(
  1203	                f"{name}: mirror_map rule id '{rid}' is not kebab-grammar "
  1204	                f"shaped (^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$)"
  1205	            )
  1206	            continue
  1207	        rule_ids.append(rid)
  1208	        sig = rule["signature"]
  1209	        if (
  1210	            not isinstance(sig, list)
    35	**Decision-home triage at row authoring (README §6; adopted
    36	2026-07-23):** every NEW-DECISION row on an off-model surface answers
    37	the K0→K4 question sequence — K0 model-shaped (→ the fix-FIRST
    38	model-wave path, user-gated; never a draft-local decision), K1–K4
    39	ADR-shaped (→ the row births its ADR under the packet-flow ADR
    40	lanes), else the row itself is the decision's home — and the outcome
    41	is recorded with the row's DECIDED-HERE marker (an ADR-born row
    42	names its ADR).
    43	
    44	A chapter may have MORE than one draft (one per memo-born surface);
    45	the chapter's draft set = the drafts its plan Packets-and-flow-mode
    46	table references.
    47	
    48	## 2. The template
    49	
    50	`v3/implementation/contracts/ch<N>-<surface>-contract.md` — the
    51	filename's chapter/surface MUST match the meta block. The file NEVER
    52	moves and row IDs NEVER change (anchors stay resolvable forever;
    53	archival is a status transition, not a relocation)
    54	(the contracts HOME was re-pinned once, `docs/v3/implementation/contracts` → `v3/implementation/contracts`, by ADR-015 — a relocation of the home as a whole, not of a file within it; filenames and row IDs unchanged, anchors unaffected).
    55	
    56	````markdown
    57	# ch<N> — <surface> contract
    58	
    59	```json
    60	{"contract_draft": {"chapter": "ch<N>", "surface": "<surface>", "status": "draft"}}
    61	```
    62	
    63	## Context (non-normative by declaration)
    64	
    65	<The Control-Model answers as round-0 context: business invariant,
    66	control model, read-path, forbidden fallback, allowed resolution,
    67	missing-data. Prose here CANNOT carry rules — an iff-clause found in
    68	prose is a review finding, never a legal edit path.>
    69	
    70	## Contract rows (every normative statement is a C-row)
    71	
    72	| ID | Rule |
    73	|---|---|
    74	| C1 | <one normative statement per row> |
    75	| C2 | … |
    76	
    77	## Ratification history (empty at `draft` — blocks are appended by the lifecycle acts)
    78	
    79	## Realized map (empty until chapter close)
    80	````
    81	
    82	The skeleton above IS the legal `draft` state: status `draft` carries
    83	NO ratification block and NO map (§3 state consistency) — a copyable
    84	one-document form showing every stage at once would be red under its
    85	own rules. The lifecycle acts APPEND:
    86	
    87	- **At ratification** (the ratifying commit — which also flips status
    88	  to `ratified`):
    89	
    90	  ```json
    91	  {"ratification": {"date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "arms": ["<arm>", "…"], "commit": "<content-commit sha>"}}
    92	  ```
    93	
    94	- **At chapter close** (the boundary review — which also flips status
    95	  to `realized`, in the SAME act; ANY map presence requires
    96	  `realized`):
    97	
    98	  ```json
    99	  {"realized_map": {"C1": "<landing: packet § / code / test>", "C2": "…"}}
   100	  ```
   101	
   102	- **At supersede** (the flip commit — which also flips status to
   103	  `superseded`, in the SAME act; ANY record presence requires
   104	  `superseded`, and the record requires it back):
   105	
   106	  ```json
   107	  {"superseded": {"date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "oracle_branch": "<branch>", "oracle_tip": "<40-hex sha>", "plan": "<repo-relative path>"}}
   108	  ```
   109	
   110	  The record answers "where did this line's state go, and under what
   111	  authority" and NOTHING else. It carries NO successor field on
   112	  purpose: the successor does not exist when the flip runs, and a
   113	  reference unverifiable at the point of writing is the measured
   114	  defect family this process already pays for (an unrun "measured",
   115	  a DERIVED claim with no named measurer, an unverified citation, a
   116	  PROJECTED state labelled measured). The forward thread is the plan
   117	  pointer; the successor surface is named where it is decided.
   118	
   119	## 3. The machine form (what the lint enforces — the full registry)
   120	
   121	**Threat model (user-ratified 2026-08-04):** every check in this
   122	registry defends against ACCIDENT and SLOPPINESS — a wrong edit, a
   123	malformed block, a stale reference, a careless anchor. None of it
   124	defends against a commit-holder's DELIBERATE concealment (rows hidden
   125	in rendering constructs, hand-crafted evasions): that class is owned
   126	by human diff review and the fresh-context arm, exactly as older
   127	ratification blocks already are ("verified by diff review, not
   128	tier 0"). A finding outside this sentence is recorded as
   129	carried-scope, never built against.
   130	
   131	- **Meta block:** exactly ONE `contract_draft` block; exact keyset
   132	  `{chapter, surface, status}`; `status` ∈ `draft | ratified |
   133	  reopened | realized | superseded`; filename
   134	  `ch<N>-<surface>-contract.md` matches chapter/surface.
   135	- **C-rows:** DISCOVERED as table rows whose FIRST cell is `C<n>`,
   136	  fenced code excluded; ids unique, NO leading zeros (`C01` is red —
   137	  ids are exact strings, `C01` is never `C1`); ratified-or-later
   138	  requires ≥1 row. Finer elements (token-list members) travel with
   139	  their host row.
   140	- **Ratification blocks:** exact keyset `{date, arms, commit}`;
   141	  `date` a `YYYY-MM-DD` string, non-decreasing in document order;
   142	  `arms` a nonempty list of nonempty strings (naming the transitional
   143	  cross-model arms that reviewed — README §5.5); `commit` 7–40
   144	  LOWERCASE-hex — shape-checked on EVERY block, while the
   145	  COMMIT-object resolution (`git cat-file -t == commit`; a tree/blob/
   146	  tag is not an auditable ratification point) and the equality check
   147	  run on the LATEST block only (= the last block in document order),
   148	  in `ratified` and `realized`. An unresolvable recorded commit or an
   149	  out-of-repo draft is a LOUD error, never a skip.
   150	- **The equality check:** the working tree's C-row lines equal the
   151	  C-row lines at the latest block's recorded commit
   152	  (`git show <commit>:<path>`). The recorded sha binds CONTENT, not
   153	  the record — the block lands in a follow-up commit. Older blocks
   154	  are human-readable history verified by diff review, not tier 0
   155	  (the stated threat model, README §5.5).
   156	- **State consistency (decidable from the current bytes alone):**
   157	  ratification block(s) present ⇔ status ∈ {ratified, reopened,
   158	  realized, superseded}; status `draft` ⇒ no blocks; the equality
   159	  check binds `ratified`/`realized`/`superseded` and is suspended
   160	  ONLY at `reopened`.
   161	- **`realized_map`:** exactly one block with this exact top-level
   162	  key; keys exactly the C-row id set; every landing site a nonempty
   163	  string; ANY map presence ⇔ status `realized` (the boundary review
   164	  fills the map and flips the status in ONE act — a partial map, on
   165	  any status, is red).
   166	- **`superseded` record:** exactly one block with this exact
   167	  top-level key; exact keyset `{date, oracle_branch, oracle_tip,
   168	  plan}`; ANY record presence ⇔ status `superseded` (both
   169	  directions), and `superseded` additionally requires ≥1
   170	  ratification block — a draft that was never ratified has no state
   171	  to preserve. `date` a `YYYY-MM-DD` string; `oracle_branch` a
   172	  resolvable branch (the local ref, else its `origin/` tracking
   173	  form); `oracle_tip` 40-hex LOWERCASE, resolving to a COMMIT object
   174	  that is CONTAINED in `oracle_branch` (`merge-base --is-ancestor`,
   175	  so later preservation commits on the oracle never break the
   176	  record); `plan` an existing repo-relative path. Every one of these
   177	  is a checkable claim about the world, checked where it is written
   178	  — the record is the machine lock, so a record whose pointers do
   179	  not resolve is a broken lock, LOUD, never a skip.
   180	- **Cross-cutting machine-block rules** (canonical statement:
   181	  `task-packet-template.md` §1a; this template defers): duplicate
   182	  JSON keys are parse errors; fences follow the line-oriented
   183	  CommonMark scanner — quoted fences are material.
   184	
   185	Anything in this template the lint cannot see is marked
   186	**panel-owned** — currently: the tree-independence bar (§1), the
   187	Context section's non-normativity, and the "every normative statement
   188	is a C-row" rule (the review-side prose-contract scan detects
   189	violations; the lint guards only the C-row byte surface).
   190	
   191	## 4. Lifecycle
   192	
   193	```
   194	draft ──(human ratification)──▶ ratified ──(chapter close)──▶ realized
   195	              ▲                    │ │                            │
   196	              └──── reopened ◀─────┴─┼────────────────────────────┘
   197	        (from ratified; from realized only as a resolved STOP; transient)
   198	                                     │
   199	                                     └──(supersede)──▶ superseded
   200	                                            (terminal — no exits)
   201	```
   202	
   203	- **First ratification (two commits):** the content commit lands the
   204	  final rows (status `draft`, no blocks — green); the ratifying
   205	  commit appends the block recording that content commit AND flips
   206	  status to `ratified` in one act (no C-row changes → equality holds
   207	  — green).
   208	- **Reopen (from `ratified` directly; from `realized` ONLY as a
   209	  human-resolved STOP — the post-close escape hatch below):** from
   210	  `ratified`: commit 1 edits the C-rows and flips status to
   211	  `reopened` (equality suspended — green); commit 2 appends the new
   212	  block recording commit 1 and flips back to `ratified`.
   213	  Re-ratification is permanently human, exactly like ratification.
   214	  The `realized` path follows the post-close bullet's mechanics
   215	  EXCLUSIVELY — its commit 2 returns to `realized`, never `ratified`.
   216	- **Post-close (realized) reopen:** a post-close change remains a
   217	  STOP; when the human resolves it by ordering a reopen (first
   218	  exercise: the ch12 ratification's mandated reopen set — ch11
   219	  C18/C19/C21/C30 + ch8-C14), the draft passes
   220	  realized → reopened → realized — commit 1 removes the
   221	  `realized_map` with the row edits and the `reopened` flip, commit 2
   222	  restores the updated map with the new block and the `realized`
   223	  flip; a reopened-to-pointer row's map entry records the historical
   224	  landing plus the delegation, realizing VACUOUSLY (the successor
   225	  surface owns the new semantics' realization at its own close);
   226	  every commit green on the draft-form rules, the packet-anchor
   227	  surface loud-red for the window (this section's own rule — the
   228	  designed signal, closed at commit 2); the resolution is never
   229	  inferred.
   230	- **Supersede (ONE commit, from `ratified` only):** when a line is
   231	  re-derived rather than repaired, the draft is archived IN PLACE —
   232	  the file never moves (§2) — by a single commit that flips status
   233	  `ratified` → `superseded` and appends the §2 record. That commit
   234	  touches the meta block and the record and NOTHING else: the C-rows
   235	  are frozen and the equality check keeps binding, forever, against
   236	  the latest ratification's recorded commit. This is deliberately
   237	  NOT the reopen's two-commit choreography — a reopen suspends
   238	  equality because the rows are being edited; a supersede edits no
   239	  row, so nothing needs suspending, and a second commit would only
   240	  create a window in which the state is half-declared.
   241	  `superseded` is TERMINAL: no transition leaves it, so the
   242	  re-derived successor is a NEW surface with its own name, never a
   243	  return to this file. Reachability from `realized` is NOT
   244	  legislated (n=1: the first exercise was the ch13 context-block
   245	  contract, superseded from `ratified` on 2026-08-03) — a
   246	  post-close supersede is a new act to be decided when one exists.
   247	  The successor's identity is not recorded here; see §2's record
   248	  form for why.
   249	- **`superseded` and the zero-reopened gate:** a superseded draft is
   250	  a PERMANENT resting state, so it does NOT join
   251	  `--forbid-reopened`; gating on it would red every future approve.
   252	  Its enforcement lives entirely at anchor resolution (§6) and in
   253	  the summary line, which names superseded drafts the way it names
   254	  reopened ones.
   255	- **`reopened` is a transient STOP-artifact, never a resting state:**
   256	  packet refs into a reopened draft go loud-red for the window;
   257	  packet approve, chapter close, and process flips require ZERO
   258	  reopened drafts (`check_packet.py --forbid-reopened`).
   259	- **Chapter close:** the boundary review fills the `realized_map` and
   260	  flips status to `realized` in ONE act. Nothing lives only in the
   261	  draft afterwards — decisions persist in ADRs, shapes persist in
   262	  packets/code/tests; the draft is never a third permanent authority.
   263	
   264	## 5. Draft metrics (one line each, at ratification and at close)
   265	
   266	Recorded in the draft's Context section tail (panel-owned, not
   267	lint-visible): rounds to ratify; new-decision row count;
   268	post-ratification reopenings (= ratification blocks beyond the
   269	first). The D2 "expected 2–3 rounds" prediction is testable only if
   270	measured.
   271	
   272	## 6. Anchoring (how packets consume a draft)
   273	
   274	Packets anchor rows as `contract:ch<N>-<surface>#C<n>` in their
   275	`packet_rows` manifest (task-packet-template §1). Anchors resolve
   276	ONLY to a **ratified-or-later** draft — neither `reopened` nor
   277	`superseded` qualifies, for opposite reasons: a reopened draft is
   278	contested (a transient red window that closes at re-ratification),
   279	a superseded one is retired (a permanent red — the anchor must move
   280	to the successor surface, and the draft's own `superseded` record
   281	names the oracle branch and the plan that authorized the move). A packet row anchored to
   282	a draft row must preserve its MEANING, not just resolve the reference
   283	— the panel's lens-2 draft→packet semantic-drift check owns that
   284	surface.

   889	    """P2's full shape rules — ONE implementation shared by the
   890	    fold-time check and the --post-build audit (the audit reads the
   891	    commit's bytes, so it must enforce the same schema itself; a
   892	    subset check over a malformed boundary proves nothing). Returns
   893	    the files list only when fully valid."""
   894	    if not isinstance(boundary, dict) or set(boundary.keys()) != MUTATION_BOUNDARY_KEYS:
   895	        checker.error(
   896	            f"{name}: mutation_boundary must be an object with exactly keys "
   897	            f"{sorted(MUTATION_BOUNDARY_KEYS)}"
   898	        )
   899	        return None
   900	    files = boundary.get("files")
   901	    if not isinstance(files, list) or not files or not all(
   902	        isinstance(f, str) and f and not f.startswith("/") and ".." not in f for f in files
   903	    ):
   904	        checker.error(
   905	            f"{name}: mutation_boundary.files must be a nonempty list of "
   906	            f"repo-relative paths"
   907	        )
   908	        return None
   909	    if len(set(files)) != len(files):
   910	        checker.error(f"{name}: mutation_boundary.files has duplicates")
   911	        return None
   912	    return files

   915	def check_ref(name: str, row_id: str, ref: object, drafts: dict[str, dict], adr_dir: Path, checker: Checker) -> None:
   916	    """P4: strict-or-prose-prefixed; anything else is loud."""
   917	    label = f"{name}: rows['{row_id}'] ref"
   918	    if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref:
   919	        checker.error(f"{label} must be a nonempty string")
   920	        return
   921	    if ref.startswith(PROSE_PREFIX):
   922	        if not ref[len(PROSE_PREFIX):].strip():
   923	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — prose: prefix with an empty remainder")
   924	        return
   925	    m = CONTRACT_REF_RE.match(ref)
   926	    if m:
   927	        slug, row = m.group(1), m.group(2)
   928	        draft = drafts.get(slug)
   929	        if draft is None:
   930	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — no such contract-draft")
   931	        elif draft["status"] not in RATIFIED_OR_LATER:
   932	            # Two out-states, opposite reasons: reopened is CONTESTED
   933	            # (a transient window, closed at re-ratification), superseded
   934	            # is RETIRED (permanent — the anchor must move to the
   935	            # successor surface, and the draft's own superseded record
   936	            # names the oracle branch and the authorizing plan).
   937	            why = ""
   938	            if draft["status"] == "reopened":
   939	                why = " (the contract is contested during a reopen)"
   940	            elif draft["status"] == "superseded":
   941	                why = (
   942	                    " — the draft is superseded (terminal): this anchor must move "
   943	                    "to the successor surface; the draft's superseded record names "
   944	                    "the oracle branch and the plan that authorized the supersede"
   945	                )
   946	            checker.error(
   947	                f"{label} '{ref}' — draft status is '{draft['status']}', anchors "
   948	                f"need ratified-or-later" + why
   949	            )
   950	        elif row not in draft["rows"]:
   951	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — row {row} not in draft")
   952	        return
   953	    m = ADR_STRICT_RE.match(ref)
   954	    if m:
   955	        if not list(adr_dir.glob(f"ADR-{m.group(1)}-*.md")):
   956	            checker.error(f"{label} '{ref}' — no such file in {adr_dir.name}/")
   957	        return
   958	    checker.error(
   959	        f"{label} '{ref}' is neither a strict form "
   960	        f"(contract:ch<N>-<surface>#C<n>, ADR-NNN) nor prose:-prefixed — "
   961	        f"in declared machine data a near-miss is loud, never reinterpreted"
   962	    )

v3/implementation/packets/ch12-p3-provider-contract.md:702:| K2 | The checks run in ONE ORDER (C18): the ADMISSION rungs FIRST — the terminal-sink STATE rung (`kernel_status ≠ TERMINAL` — the ratified model fix `76e34413`, mirroring FAIL: a late READY after CANCEL/FAIL must not resurrect the run), then the CORRELATION rung (`runtime_context = requested(request_id)` — the readiness for the request WE issued); a rung-rejected event mutates NOTHING and the later checks never run (a bare-REQUIRE guard rejection). THEN the ref's TRANSPORT gate (canonical-JSON-safety, PR4 — breach = integrity throw), THEN the `requirement = required(spec)` BIND (the verbatim unit's `REQUIRE requirement = required(spec)` — a made-explicit integrity guard that ALSO binds `spec` before `spec.kind` is read; the K2/K4 split is a matrix decomposition, so a build binds `spec` via the `required(spec)` destructure at THIS point, not after the kind check), THEN the ONLY business validation: the kind boundary `ref.kind = spec.kind` — a MISMATCH is a bare-REQUIRE guard rejection, NO state change, the ref NOT accepted; the LOCATOR is provider-defined and NEVER validated (`kind-boundary-only`) (anchored: contract:ch12-runtime-core#C18, #C15 + prose:l0e-pseudocode/RUNTIME_CONTEXT_READY + prose:ledger §2 l0e/kind-boundary-only) |

v3/implementation/packets/ch12-p3-provider-contract.md:710:| SM1 | The completion seam is ORDERED-AFTER-COMMIT (C15, DECIDED HERE in the draft — the model's "async → later fires" strengthened into a binding composition-seam rule): the composition delivers a READY completion into RUNTIME_CONTEXT_READY only AFTER the provisioning START's atomic commit has landed. The seam has TWO temporal paths (both ordered-after-commit; SM2's never-dropped covers both): (a) a completion RACING the pre-conclusion window — a provider completing SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `provision()` (the scripted player may), or asynchronously BEFORE the START attempt concludes — is HELD by the seam, never lost to the pre-commit correlation window (`runtime_context` still `none` would reject it), and released at conclusion (SM2); (b) a completion arriving AFTER the START attempt has CONCLUDED — the NORMAL async path, the commit ALREADY landed — is delivered DIRECTLY into RUNTIME_CONTEXT_READY, not held (correlation matches on the committed marker, or inert-rejects for a superseded id). The hold exists ONLY for path (a); path (b) is the common case (a real provider provisions asynchronously and fires READY long after START returns) (anchored: contract:ch12-runtime-core#C15) |
v3/implementation/packets/ch12-p3-provider-contract.md:711:| SM2 | The hold's RELEASE rule: a held completion releases when the initiating START attempt CONCLUDES — either its commit landed (delivery proceeds, correlation matches) or the attempt failed/was superseded (delivery still proceeds and the correlation rung rejects it, inert); a held completion is NEVER dropped silently and NEVER delivered mid-attempt. The seam's HOLD/enqueue returns to the provider IMMEDIATELY (holding never blocks the completion call), so no circular wait exists with C18's detach-acknowledgment await (anchored: contract:ch12-runtime-core#C15) |
v3/implementation/packets/ch12-p3-provider-contract.md:712:| SM3 | The seam's BINDING property (entailed by SM1/SM2) is CONCLUSION-SIGNALLED DELIVERY: a held completion is delivered ONLY when the initiating START attempt CONCLUDES (commit-landed, or failed/superseded) — NOT on any event-loop scheduling primitive. The EXCLUDED anti-pattern is a `queueMicrotask`/`setTimeout` delivery, because a provider completing synchronously inside `provision()` posts its READY while START's own post-`await` continuation (which runs `commitLifecycle`) is still queued behind it — a microtask FIFO would deliver READY BEFORE the `requested(request_id)` marker commits, and the correlation rung (still `none`) would reject it, LOSING the completion (the exact hazard SM1 forbids). The REFERENCE realization is an explicit per-`request_id` in-memory buffer flushed at the attempt's conclusion; any conclusion-gated EQUIVALENT (e.g. a per-`request_id` latch/promise resolved at `concludeAttempt`) is admissible PROVIDED delivery is triggered ONLY by attempt-conclusion — the constraint is the property, not the data structure. What is FORECLOSED is only the scheduling-based delivery: it satisfies neither SM1 nor SM2 and would rest on event-loop ORDERING substrate this packet deliberately does not stand on (reconciling the "no substrate probe required" line — a conclusion-signalled delivery is deterministic in-memory, the microtask variant is the excluded substrate dependency). The buffer/delivery is a COMPOSITION-INJECTED seam (C15's "the composition delivers"; ADR-014's port-injection culture — injected into the kernel like the registry, the store uninvolved — Mutable-flow record); only the CONCLUSION SIGNAL is kernel-originated: the `request_id` is minted inside `start` and NOT surfaced on `StartOutcome`, so `start` signals `concludeAttempt` at EACH provisioning attempt's conclusion. The TWO-PATH realization (SM1): the seam carries a `concluded` SET, marked at `concludeAttempt` BEFORE the buffer flush — a completion arriving while the id is UN-concluded is BUFFERED (path a, flushed at `concludeAttempt`); a completion arriving after the id is CONCLUDED is delivered DIRECTLY (path b — `deliverCompletion` invokes the RUNTIME_CONTEXT_READY handler, DETACHED so it returns to the provider IMMEDIATELY per SM2, the commit already landed so correlation matches or inert-rejects). The detached direct deliveries are tracked for a DRAIN (`Kernel.settleRuntimeContextDeliveries()` awaits every in-flight direct delivery and RETURNS their outcomes — a delivered-inert completion yields `{ignored}`, a dropped one yields nothing: the fail-able distinction SM2 needs; a real shutdown drains before teardown). The release TRIGGER is PER-ATTEMPT (C15/C18: a held completion releases when ITS initiating attempt concludes — commit-landed, failed, or SUPERSEDED), not per-`start`-call: on the CAS-restart (S5) a superseded `request_id`'s attempt CONCLUDES at the restart, and its buffered completion is released (inert by correlation, K3) THERE — at supersession, as the per-attempt trigger requires, BEFORE the next attempt provisions. The call-end `try/finally` over the restart loop is ONLY the leak-proof BACKSTOP — it flushes any minted id NOT already released at its own attempt's conclusion (the terminal committed/failed attempt, and a defensive net against an early throw), never the primary release for a superseded id. The post-provision exits `concludeAttempt` covers (the `try/finally` over the restart loop flushes EVERY minted id on each): `committed`, `duplicate_op`, `op_id_collision`, a THROWING `commitLifecycle` (a store-port rejection — the `finally` covers it), and the S4 `provision` port-breach throw; one `start` call may mint MULTIPLE `request_id`s (the S5 CAS-restart re-provisions under a fresh id), each buffered and each released at its own attempt's conclusion (the superseded id delivers-and-fails-correlation, inert — K3) (derived: contract:ch12-runtime-core#C15, #C18 + prose:ADR-014 composition-injected seam — DERIVATION: C15's FIVE observable rules (held, released-on-conclude, never-lost, never-mid-attempt, immediate-return) FORECLOSE the scheduling realization — a microtask post loses the pre-commit completion, so CONCLUSION-SIGNALLED DELIVERY is entailed (the property, not a specific data structure — an explicit buffer is the reference representation, a conclusion-gated latch an equivalent; the D1-grain representation choice is the build's); the request_id conclusion signal and the multi-id CAS-restart flush are S5/C18's own arithmetic) |

  1772	def run_lint(
  1773	    packets_dir: Path,
  1774	    contracts_dir: Path,
  1775	    adr_dir: Path,
  1776	    forbid_reopened: bool = False,
  1777	) -> int:
  1778	    errors, stats = lint(packets_dir, contracts_dir, adr_dir, forbid_reopened=forbid_reopened)
  1779	    for msg in errors:
  1780	        print(f"packet-lint FAIL: {msg}", file=sys.stderr)
  1781	    reopened = stats["reopened"]
  1782	    reopened_note = f"{len(reopened)} reopened" + (f": {', '.join(reopened)}" if reopened else "")
  1783	    superseded = stats["superseded"]
  1784	    superseded_note = f"{len(superseded)} superseded" + (
  1785	        f": {', '.join(superseded)}" if superseded else ""
  1786	    )
  1787	    print(
  1788	        f"packet-lint: {stats['v2']} v2 packet(s) linted, "
  1789	        f"{stats['grandfathered']} pre-v2 grandfathered, "
  1790	        f"{stats['drafts']} draft(s) linted "
  1791	        f"({reopened_note}, {superseded_note}), "
  1792	        f"{len(errors)} error(s)"
  1793	    )
  1794	    return 1 if errors else 0

  1854	GIT_BASE = ["git", "-c", "user.email=lint@selftest", "-c", "user.name=lint-selftest"]

  1856	
  1857	def _git_ok(root: Path, *argv: str) -> bool:
  1858	    return subprocess.run(GIT_BASE + list(argv), cwd=root, capture_output=True).returncode == 0
  1859	
  1860	
  1861	def _git_out(root: Path, *argv: str) -> str:
  1862	    return subprocess.run(
  1863	        GIT_BASE + list(argv), cwd=root, capture_output=True, text=True
  1864	    ).stdout.strip()
  1865	
  1866	
  1867	def build_green_fixture() -> tuple[tempfile.TemporaryDirectory, Path, Path, Path, str] | None:
  1868	    """A git-initialized fixture enacting the ratification choreography:
  1869	    commit the draft content (status draft, no blocks), then record that
  1870	    commit in a ratification block + flip to ratified. Returns
  1871	    (tmpdir, root, draft_path, packet_path, ratified_draft_text)."""
  1872	    tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
  1873	    root = Path(tmp.name)
  1874	    (root / "packets").mkdir()
  1875	    cdir = root / "contracts"
  1876	    cdir.mkdir()
  1877	    draft = cdir / "ch9-test-surface-contract.md"
  1878	    draft.write_text(DRAFT_V1, encoding="utf-8")
  1879	    if not (_git_ok(root, "init", "-q") and _git_ok(root, "add", "-A") and _git_ok(root, "commit", "-q", "-m", "content")):
  1880	        tmp.cleanup()
  1881	        return None
  1882	    sha = _git_out(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
  1883	    ratified = DRAFT_V1.replace('"status": "draft"', '"status": "ratified"') + RATIFICATION_TMPL.replace(
  1884	        "%DATE%", "2026-07-09"
  1885	    ).replace("%COMMIT%", sha)
  1886	    draft.write_text(ratified, encoding="utf-8")
  1887	    if not (_git_ok(root, "add", "-A") and _git_ok(root, "commit", "-q", "-m", "record")):
  1888	        tmp.cleanup()
  1889	        return None
  1890	    packet = root / "packets" / "ch9-p1-test.md"
  1891	    packet.write_text(GREEN_PACKET, encoding="utf-8")
  1892	    return tmp, root, draft, packet, ratified
  1893	
  1894	
  1895	def build_superseded_fixture() -> tuple[tempfile.TemporaryDirectory, Path, Path, Path, str, str] | None:
  1896	    """The supersede act enacted on the green fixture: a preserved line
  1897	    (a branch whose tip carries the plan), then ONE commit flipping
  1898	    ratified -> superseded and appending the record — C-rows untouched,
  1899	    so the equality lock holds across the flip. Returns (tmpdir, root,
  1900	    draft_path, packet_path, superseded_text, off_branch_sha); the last
  1901	    is the flip commit itself, which is by construction NOT contained in
  1902	    the oracle branch (D8.4's containment negative)."""
  1903	    fixture = build_green_fixture()
  1904	    if fixture is None:
  1905	        return None
  1906	    tmp, root, draft, packet, green = fixture
  1907	    (root / ORACLE_PLAN).write_text("the authorizing re-derivation plan\n", encoding="utf-8")
  1908	    if not (
  1909	        _git_ok(root, "add", "-A")
  1910	        and _git_ok(root, "commit", "-q", "-m", "plan")
  1911	        and _git_ok(root, "branch", ORACLE_BRANCH)
  1912	    ):
  1913	        tmp.cleanup()
  1914	        return None
  1915	    tip = _git_out(root, "rev-parse", ORACLE_BRANCH)
  1916	    text = green.replace('"status": "ratified"', '"status": "superseded"') + (
  1917	        SUPERSEDED_TMPL.replace("%BRANCH%", ORACLE_BRANCH)
  1918	        .replace("%TIP%", tip)
  1919	        .replace("%PLAN%", ORACLE_PLAN)
  1920	    )
  1921	    draft.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
  1922	    if not (_git_ok(root, "add", "-A") and _git_ok(root, "commit", "-q", "-m", "supersede")):
  1923	        tmp.cleanup()
  1924	        return None
  1925	    return tmp, root, draft, packet, text, _git_out(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
  1926

  1928	def run_selftest() -> int:
  1929	    failures: list[str] = []
  1930	    red_dims: list[str] = []
  1931	
  1932	    shared = build_green_fixture()
  1933	    if shared is None:
  1934	        print("selftest FAIL: git fixture setup failed", file=sys.stderr)
  1935	        return 1
  1936	    shared_tmp, shared_root, shared_draft, shared_packet, green_draft = shared
  1937	
  1938	    def assert_red(name: str, errors: list[str], substr: str) -> None:
  1939	        """A dim is red for ITS OWN claim, or it proves nothing — the
  1940	        exit code alone let an unrelated error (e.g. a broken fixture
  1941	        sha) mask a dead check."""
  1942	        red_dims.append(name)
  1943	        if not any(substr in e for e in errors):
  1944	            failures.append(f"selftest dim NOT red for its claim: {name} (no error containing '{substr}')")
  1945	
  3160	            and _git_ok(root, "commit", "-q", "-m", "migrate")
  3161	        )
  3162	        if not ok:
  3163	            failures.append("selftest git fixture setup failed (adr-015 alias d5)")
  3164	        else:
  3165	            new_draft = new_cdir / "ch9-test-surface-contract.md"

  3170	                failures.append(
  3171	                    f"selftest green NOT green: alias-d5-ancestor ({checker.errors[0]})"
  3172	                )
  3173	            checker = Checker()
  3174	            check_recorded_equality(new_draft, content_sha, rows, checker, migration_parent=None)
  3175	            assert_red("alias-d5-unset-loud", checker.errors, "ratification record is broken")
  3176	
  3177	    # ---- the green pair must pass
  3178	    errors, _ = lint(shared_root / "packets", shared_root / "contracts", ADR_DIR)
  3179	    expect_green("GREEN fixture pair", errors)
  3180	    shared_tmp.cleanup()
  3181	
  3182	    for f in failures:
  3183	        print(f"selftest FAIL: {f}", file=sys.stderr)
  3184	    print(
  3185	        f"selftest: {len(red_dims)} red dims exercised, green fixture + reopen "
  3186	        f"choreography + fenced-noise greens pass, {len(failures)} failure(s)"
  3187	    )
  3188	    return 1 if failures else 0

  3192	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)

  3194	    parser.add_argument("--packets-dir", type=Path, default=PACKETS_DIR)

  3200	    args = parser.parse_args()
  3201

# ch13 — context-block contract

```json
{"contract_draft": {"chapter": "ch13", "surface": "context-block", "status": "ratified"}}
```

## Context (non-normative by declaration)

**Business invariant.** L2 enforces; L2b communicates. A context block
is deterministic, actor-facing prose rendered into the dispatched
ContextPacket so the actor is told an operating rule before acting —
it never enters a verdict, a gate decision, or any kernel decision
surface (`l2b/communication-only`, disposition `review`; the precise
byte-scope is C12's).

**Control model.** One render mechanism, two ref sources, one body
source: the template-level catalog holds every body; role/step
prompt-concern refs (the L0c slot this chapter first resolves) and
gate/policy block refs are id lists into it. Refs are validated at
ADMISSION-time definition-static checking (the definition-issue
channel, static analog of binding coverage; the walk/rung realization
split is C19's); the render is a pure function at dispatch.

**Read-path, staged.** The definition-static lanes read the SOURCE
form (the walk) and the built template (the admission rung), per
C19's split; `assemble_context_blocks(instance, template, step)`
reads the admitted template's NORMALIZED fields and catalog (C17) —
never the merged effective agent config (C5).

**Forbidden fallback.** No render-time drop of an unresolved ref and
no render-time skip of a malformed catalog (neither state exists
post-admission — C7/C9/C17); no code-invented rejection name (the
issue code is the EXISTING `unresolved_context_block_ref` from the
ch11 model-sync 31-name definition-issue family).

**Allowed resolution.** An absent catalog with zero refs issued is
the every-existing-file state — fully legal, zero-impact.

**Missing-data.** A template with refs but no resolving catalog
entries fails at definition-static checking (per-site findings, C7).

**Model-table note (superseded cells, recorded — no divergence stop
owed):** the model's Canonical Context Block Contract table still
says "reject at create" on its two ref rows; the same section's
invariant record (`refs-validated-at-definition-load`: "runs under
ADMISSION"), the `CREATE_INSTANCE` reprint's own comment, and the
Evidence block all say ADMISSION — the table cells are pre-model-fix
residue of the ch11 sync (definition-static validation moved to the
admission channel). This draft follows ADMISSION.

**Substrate probes (recorded per the ch11 probe-record form —
yaml@2.9.0, the v3 package's pinned dependency; YAML 1.2 semantics
via `YAML.parseDocument`):**
- **PROBE-CB1 (2026-07-26; re-executed at the round-2 and round-3
  panels):** kebab-case catalog keys with digit-bearing segments
  (`no-converged-before-round-2`) are plain STRINGS at node level; a
  bare digit-leading key (`2`) is a NUMBER at node level — the GP2
  identity class.
- **PROBE-CB2 (2026-07-26; re-executed at the round-2 and round-3
  panels):** a bare `true:` key is a BOOLEAN at node level; beside
  the string key `"true":` the pair passes the duplicate-key gate
  (the loader's `yamlNodeEqual` is SameValueZero on scalar values —
  `true ≠ "true"`). Under a NAKED default `toJS` the pair would
  collapse to one key (last wins) — but the v3 loader resolves with
  `toJS({ mapAsMap: true })` EXACTLY to prevent that collapse (its
  own comment says so), so non-string keys ARRIVE PRESERVED at the
  walk, and every sibling open-key surface (`steps`, `roles`,
  `transitions`, the gates subtree) already carries a WALK-side
  non-string-key validation lane reporting at the nearest
  addressable path. The consequence for this chapter is C2's key
  lane in the walk — the sibling pattern applied to the new open-key
  map.
- **PROBE-CB3 (2026-07-26, executed at the round-2 panel through the
  real `loadTemplate`; EXTENDED at the round-3 panel):** integer-like
  keys authored in QUOTED form (`"2":`) admit as record keys and
  HOIST to the front of the record's enumeration order (`transitions`
  authored `ZZZ, "2", AAA` → admitted keys `["2","ZZZ","AAA"]`, and
  the rebuilt gates record mirrors it); MULTIPLE integer-like keys
  additionally re-sort ASCENDING among themselves (authored
  `ZZZ,"10","2",AAA,"1"` → `["1","2","10","ZZZ","AAA"]` — the
  round-3 measurement); an UNQUOTED `2:` is rejected upstream as a
  non-string key. C9's order basis rests on this measured behavior.

**Prepared reopen texts (ch11-C4 + ch11-C30 + the ch11-C41
comparative parenthetical — ONE post-close reopen act over three
named sites, riding this draft's ratification; the act's carrier row
is C16; the realized→reopened→realized mechanics per the
contract-draft template §4):**
- C4 becomes: *"A gate map is FIXED-KEYSET: `uses` (required) +
  `config` (optional map) + `contextBlockRefs` (optional — value
  class and semantics owned by `contract:ch13-context-block#C6`). NO
  `implementation` / `execution` / `id` keys exist — the
  implementation and execution axes are evaluator-INTRINSIC
  (registry-resolved from `uses`), never authored; unknown keys are
  ADMISSION issues per the ch8-C13 fail-closed culture."*
- C30's trailing `context_block_refs` clause (semicolon-joined — the
  edit also closes the preceding clause with a period) becomes:
  *"The authored gate-binding key `contextBlockRefs` (the model's
  `context_block_refs`) joined the binding keyset at the ch13
  ratification act — value class and semantics:
  `contract:ch13-context-block#C6`; before ch13 it was explicitly
  not a key of this surface."* (Only that clause changes; the row's
  remaining text is untouched.)
- C41's comparative parenthetical is edited IN FULL (both mentions —
  the half-fix would leave the antecedent naming a pattern the same
  act retires): *"deliberately NOT the then-standing C30
  `context_block_refs` non-surface pattern: `context_block_refs` was
  another chapter's semantics (landed at ch13 as the authored key
  `contextBlockRefs` — `contract:ch13-context-block#C6`), while the override is ratified
  model capability on THIS surface"*.
- Map-entry duties, ITEMIZED (the token-blind `v3:realized-map` lint
  cannot catch these — the ch9-C27 lesson): C4's entry updates its
  keyset parenthetical — *"(uses + optional config)"* becomes
  *"(uses + optional config; + contextBlockRefs at ch13 —
  delegation: ch13#C6)"* — beside its historical landing; C30's
  entry RETIRES the code witness *"context_block_refs is NOT a key
  (validate.ts unknown-key)"* and records the delegation (the
  witness's intent is falsified by ch13-P1 by design — and the
  witness was mis-sited anyway: the binding unknown-key lane lives
  in the admission rung, not validate.ts); C41's entry is
  annotation-only (the clause fix). All three realize VACUOUSLY
  (this chapter's close owns the new key's realization).

**Reopen-vs-aggregate-note criterion (applied here; promotion is a
NAMED boundary-review item — plan §13.5(e) is the carrier):** a row
is REOPENED when its operative text is a hard promise about THIS
surface (ch11-C4's closed keyset; ch11-C30's "explicitly not a key";
C41's present-tense comparative); it is AGGREGATE-NOTED when its own
text pre-authorizes or forward-scopes the arrival (ch12-C7's
delegation clause names this chapter; ch12-C1's aggregate-note chain;
ch9-C18's "the later ContextAssembly surface (L2b, the named §1.3
candidate)" — aggregate-noted here: its "later" clause resolves to
this chapter, recorded without a reopen). Tie-break, decided by the
live case: a row carrying BOTH a hard promise AND a delegation clause
(ch12-C7) follows the DELEGATION — the promise is self-scoped to the
pre-arrival state by the row's own next clause. Fourth application,
recorded: ch11-C21's lane matrix is SELF-SCOPING ("the containers
this surface introduces"; its checklist derivation is its own P4's) —
its text stays true after the act (the `gate-map unknown key` lane is
parametric on C4's keyset), the ch13 binding-level lanes are C19's
inventory, and the reopened C4's delegation pointer carries
discoverability: aggregate-noted, no reopen.

**Seed-row disposition (every §13.3 item landed — the ch8–ch12 form;
no seed item is undisposed):** the three keys' camelCase spelling +
attachment points + keyset rules → C1/C3/C4/C6; the catalog entry's
fixed keyset + unknown-key disposition → C3; the packet TS shape +

step+event pair → C11; the admission finding's lane (path form,

case) → C8 + C9; the render when a gate ref's binding is not
offerable → C10; the shipped catalog entry's PRESENCE rule → C14;
the REQUIRED caveat row → C15. Rows beyond the seed (C2, C5, C12's byte-scope precision, C13,
C16, C17, C18, C19) per §13.3's "the draft decides".

**Decision-home triage (template §1, the K0→K4 sequence — outcomes
for ALL TWELVE DECIDED-HERE markers: C2 key lane + grammar, C3
no-reserve, C6 any-binding, C7 entry-belted resolution grain (added

  1489	        checker.error(f"{name}: packet_metrics unknown keys {sorted(unknown)}")
  1490	
  1491	    def type_err(field: str, expected: str) -> None:
  1492	        checker.error(f"{name}: packet_metrics.{field} must be {expected}")
  1493	
  1494	    if "class" in metrics and not isinstance(metrics["class"], str):
  1495	        type_err("class", "a string")

  1506	            type_err("prediction", "an object")

  1509	                type_err("prediction", f"an object with exactly keys {sorted(PREDICTION_KEYS)}")

  1522	        else:
  1523	            for key, value in provenance.items():
  1524	                if not _is_int(value) or value < 0:
  1525	                    type_err(f"provenance.{key}", "a nonnegative integer")

  1532	    rounds = metrics.get("rounds")

  1535	            type_err("rounds", f"an object with exactly keys {sorted(ROUNDS_KEYS)}")
  1536	        else:
  1537	            for key, value in rounds.items():
  1538	                if not _is_int(value) or value < 0:
  1539	                    type_err(f"rounds.{key}", "a nonnegative integer — these are counters")
  1540	
  1541	    stops = metrics.get("stops")
  1542	    if "stops" in metrics:
  1543	        if not isinstance(stops, list):
  1544	            type_err("stops", "a list")
  1545	        else:
  1546	            for stop in stops:
  1547	                if not isinstance(stop, dict) or set(stop.keys()) != {"type", "what", "resolution"}:
  1548	                    type_err("stops[]", "objects with exactly type/what/resolution")

  1561	                        type_err(f"stops[].{key}", "a string")
  1562	
  1563	    misses = metrics.get("detector_misses")
  1564	    if "detector_misses" in metrics:
  1565	        if not isinstance(misses, list):
  1566	            type_err("detector_misses", "a list")

  1576	                    type_err("detector_misses[].found_at", f"one of {sorted(FOUND_AT_VALUES)}")

  1579	def check_post_build(

  1382	def check_packet(
  1383	    path: Path,
  1384	    drafts: dict[str, dict],
  1385	    adr_dir: Path,
  1386	    checker: Checker,
  1387	) -> bool:
  1388	    """Returns True iff the packet is v2 (and was linted)."""
  1389	    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  1390	    # Grandfathering must be decided BEFORE any error is reported: a
  1391	    # WHITELISTED pre-v2 packet with an unrelated malformed fence is
  1392	    # SKIPPED, not failed. The reverse hole is closed textually — a
  1393	    # packet whose mutation_boundary fence itself is malformed still
  1394	    # counts as v2 (the raw text names the marker), so it cannot
  1395	    # silently demote itself to grandfathered. And grandfathering is a
  1396	    # CLOSED set: an unmarked packet OUTSIDE the whitelist is red, not
  1397	    # skipped — a future packet cannot dodge its v2 obligations.
  1398	    probe = Checker()

  1402	        if path.name in GRANDFATHERED_PACKETS:
  1403	            return False  # pre-v2: grandfathered, parse noise suppressed
  1404	        checker.error(
  1405	            f"{path.name}: post-v2 packet without the mutation_boundary "
  1406	            f"machine block — v2 obligations bind from ch7-P3 onward; "
  1407	            f"grandfathering is closed"
  1408	        )
  1409	        return True
  1410	    checker.errors.extend(probe.errors)
  1411	    if not boundaries:

  1413	            f"{path.name}: names mutation_boundary but no parseable "
  1414	            f"mutation_boundary block found"
  1415	        )
  1416	        return True
  1417	    if len(boundaries) > 1:
  1418	        checker.error(f"{path.name}: {len(boundaries)} mutation_boundary blocks; exactly one allowed")
  1419	
  1420	    check_boundary_files(path.name, boundaries[0], checker)
  1421	
  1422	    prose = strip_fences(text)
  1423	
  1424	    # P6: the withdrawn carrier may not reappear.

   221	at a retired home would otherwise pass while checking nothing.

  1928	def run_selftest() -> int:
  1929	    failures: list[str] = []
  1930	    red_dims: list[str] = []
  1931	
  1932	    shared = build_green_fixture()
  1933	    if shared is None:
  1934	        print("selftest FAIL: git fixture setup failed", file=sys.stderr)
  1935	        return 1
  1936	    shared_tmp, shared_root, shared_draft, shared_packet, green_draft = shared
  1937	
  1938	    def assert_red(name: str, errors: list[str], substr: str) -> None:
  1939	        """A dim is red for ITS OWN claim, or it proves nothing — the
  1940	        exit code alone let an unrelated error (e.g. a broken fixture
  1941	        sha) mask a dead check."""
  1942	        red_dims.append(name)
  1943	        if not any(substr in e for e in errors):
  1944	            failures.append(f"selftest dim NOT red for its claim: {name} (no error containing '{substr}')")

import importlib.util, json
from pathlib import Path
p=Path('tools/v3-plan/check_packet.py').resolve(); s=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m',p); m=importlib.util.module_from_spec(s); s.loader.exec_module(m)
packets=sorted((Path('v3/implementation/packets')).glob('*.md'))
rows=[]
for f in packets:
    if f.name=='README.md': continue
    t=f.read_text(); prose,raws=m._scan_fences(t)
    blocks=[]
    for raw in raws:
        try:
            d=json.loads(raw)
            if isinstance(d,dict): blocks.append(d)
        except Exception: pass
    v2=any('packet_rows' in b for b in blocks)
    headers=[(x.group(1),m.HEADER_UNION_ROW_RE.findall(x.group(2))) for x in m.HEADER_UNION_RE.finditer(prose)]
    manifest=[]
    for b in blocks:
        pr=b.get('packet_rows')
        if isinstance(pr,dict) and isinstance(pr.get('rows'),list):
            for row in pr['rows']:
                if isinstance(row,dict) and isinstance(row.get('refs'),list):
                    manifest += [r for r in row['refs'] if isinstance(r,str) and r.startswith('contract:')]
    if headers or manifest:
        rows.append((f.name,v2,len(headers),len(manifest)))
print('packets with contract header-union or manifest refs:')
for row in rows: print('  %s v2=%s header_unions=%d manifest_contract_refs=%d'%row)
print('totals: files=%d v2=%d header_union_files=%d grandfathered_with_contract_forms=%d'%(

+import importlib.util
+from pathlib import Path
+
+LINTER = Path('/Users/felho/dev/pairflow/tools/v3-plan/check_packet.py')
+spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('d8lint', LINTER)
+m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+spec.loader.exec_module(m)
+
+def run(name, draft_mut=lambda t, off: t, packet_mut=None, remove_packet=True, commit=False):
+    fx = m.build_superseded_fixture()
+    assert fx is not None
+    tmp, root, draft, packet, text, off = fx
+    draft.write_text(draft_mut(text, off), encoding='utf-8')
+    if remove_packet:
+        packet.unlink()
+    elif packet_mut:
+        packet.write_text(packet_mut(packet.read_text(encoding='utf-8')), encoding='utf-8')
+    if commit:
+        assert m._git_ok(root, 'add', '-A') and m._git_ok(root, 'commit', '-q', '-m', name)
+    errors, stats = m.lint(root/'packets', root/'contracts', m.ADR_DIR)
+    print(f'{name}: ' + ('GREEN' if not errors else f'RED[{len(errors)}] ' + ' || '.join(errors)))
+    tmp.cleanup()
+
+run('date-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": ""'))
+run('date-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": 20260803'))
+run('date-impossible-calendar', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": "2026-02-31"'))
+run('date-escaped-final-newline', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": "2026-08-03\\n"'))
+run('branch-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": ""'))
+run('branch-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": []'))
+run('branch-no-such', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": "no-such"'))
+run('tip-empty', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"','"oracle_tip": ""',t))
+run('tip-wrong-type', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"','"oracle_tip": null',t))
+run('tip-uppercase', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "([0-9a-f]{40})"',lambda x:'"oracle_tip": "'+x.group(1).upper()+'"',t))
+run('tip-off-branch', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"',f'"oracle_tip": "{o}"',t))
+run('plan-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": ""'))
+run('plan-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": {}'))

- **Ratification blocks:** exact keyset `{date, arms, commit}`;
  `date` a `YYYY-MM-DD` string, non-decreasing in document order;
  `arms` a nonempty list of nonempty strings (naming the transitional
  cross-model arms that reviewed — README §5.5); `commit` 7–40
  LOWERCASE-hex — shape-checked on EVERY block, while the
  COMMIT-object resolution (`git cat-file -t == commit`; a tree/blob/
  tag is not an auditable ratification point) and the equality check
  run on the LATEST block only (= the last block in document order),
  in `ratified` and `realized`. An unresolvable recorded commit or an
  out-of-repo draft is a LOUD error, never a skip.
- **The equality check:** the working tree's C-row lines equal the
  C-row lines at the latest block's recorded commit
  (`git show <commit>:<path>`). The recorded sha binds CONTENT, not
  the record — the block lands in a follow-up commit. Older blocks
  are human-readable history verified by diff review, not tier 0
  (the stated threat model, README §5.5).
- **State consistency (decidable from the current bytes alone):**
  ratification block(s) present ⇔ status ∈ {ratified, reopened,
  realized, superseded}; status `draft` ⇒ no blocks; the equality
  check binds `ratified`/`realized`/`superseded` and is suspended
  ONLY at `reopened`.
EQUALITY_BOUND = ('ratified', 'realized', 'superseded')
- **Supersede (ONE commit, from `ratified` only):** when a line is
  re-derived rather than repaired, the draft is archived IN PLACE —
  the file never moves (§2) — by a single commit that flips status
  `ratified` → `superseded` and appends the §2 record. That commit
  touches the meta block and the record and NOTHING else: the C-rows
  are frozen and the equality check keeps binding, forever, against
  the latest ratification's recorded commit. This is deliberately
  NOT the reopen's two-commit choreography — a reopen suspends
  equality because the rows are being edited; a supersede edits no
  row, so nothing needs suspending, and a second commit would only
  create a window in which the state is half-declared.
  `superseded` is TERMINAL: no transition leaves it, so the
  re-derived successor is a NEW surface with its own name, never a
  return to this file. Reachability from `realized` is NOT
  legislated (n=1: the first exercise was the ch13 context-block
  contract, superseded from `ratified` on 2026-08-03) — a
  post-close supersede is a new act to be decided when one exists.
  The successor's identity is not recorded here; see §2's record

+import importlib.util
+from pathlib import Path
+
+LINTER = Path('/Users/felho/dev/pairflow/tools/v3-plan/check_packet.py')
+spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('d8lint', LINTER)
+m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+spec.loader.exec_module(m)
+
+def run(name, draft_mut=lambda t, off: t, packet_mut=None, remove_packet=True, commit=False):
+    fx = m.build_superseded_fixture()
+    assert fx is not None
+    tmp, root, draft, packet, text, off = fx
+    draft.write_text(draft_mut(text, off), encoding='utf-8')
+    if remove_packet:
+        packet.unlink()
+    elif packet_mut:
+        packet.write_text(packet_mut(packet.read_text(encoding='utf-8')), encoding='utf-8')
+    if commit:
+        assert m._git_ok(root, 'add', '-A') and m._git_ok(root, 'commit', '-q', '-m', name)
+    errors, stats = m.lint(root/'packets', root/'contracts', m.ADR_DIR)
+    print(f'{name}: ' + ('GREEN' if not errors else f'RED[{len(errors)}] ' + ' || '.join(errors)))
+    tmp.cleanup()
+
+run('date-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": ""'))
+run('date-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": 20260803'))
+run('date-impossible-calendar', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": "2026-02-31"'))
+run('date-escaped-final-newline', lambda t,o:t.replace('"date": "2026-08-03"','"date": "2026-08-03\\n"'))
+run('branch-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": ""'))
+run('branch-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": []'))
+run('branch-no-such', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"oracle_branch": "{m.ORACLE_BRANCH}"','"oracle_branch": "no-such"'))
+run('tip-empty', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"','"oracle_tip": ""',t))
+run('tip-wrong-type', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"','"oracle_tip": null',t))
+run('tip-uppercase', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "([0-9a-f]{40})"',lambda x:'"oracle_tip": "'+x.group(1).upper()+'"',t))
+run('tip-off-branch', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'"oracle_tip": "[0-9a-f]{40}"',f'"oracle_tip": "{o}"',t))
+run('plan-empty', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": ""'))
+run('plan-wrong-type', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": {}'))
+run('plan-absolute', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": "/tmp/plan.md"'))
+run('plan-missing', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": "no-such.md"'))
+run('plan-normalizing-dotdot', lambda t,o:t.replace(f'"plan": "{m.ORACLE_PLAN}"','"plan": "contracts/../plan-fixture.md"'))
+run('record-wrong-type', lambda t,o:__import__('re').sub(r'\{"superseded": \{[^\n]+\}\}', '{"superseded": []}', t))
+run('row-semantic-edit', lambda t,o:t.replace('| C1 | the row |','| C1 | changed |'), commit=True)
+run('row-trailing-space-edit', lambda t,o:t.replace('| C1 | the row |','| C1 | the row |   '), commit=True)
+run('realized-map-on-superseded', lambda t,o:t+'\n```json\n{"realized_map":{"C1":"x","C2":"y"}}\n```\n')
+run('manifest-anchor', remove_packet=False)
+run('header-union-only', remove_packet=False, packet_mut=lambda t:t.replace('"contract:ch9-test-surface#C1"','"prose:header-c1"').replace('"contract:ch9-test-surface#C2", ','"prose:header-c2", ')+'\nContract anchors: `contract:ch9-test-surface` rows C1/C2\n')
+

+errors,stats=m.lint(root/'packets',root/'contracts',m.ADR_DIR)
+print(f'grandfathered-header-union-only: ' + ('GREEN' if not errors else 'RED '+ ' || '.join(errors)) + f' (grandfathered={stats["grandfathered"]})')
+tmp.cleanup()

+print('forbid-reopened-with-superseded: '+('GREEN' if not errors else 'RED '+ ' || '.join(errors)))
